anomalyco/opencode ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v2.2.6 Stable2026-11-06T23:37:20Z

Highlights

  • Shared MCP Connections: Turn on pooling for an MCP server to share one connection across projects with the same setup, so you run fewer processes and start faster. (#61185)
  • GUI Plugin Themes and New Slots: GUI plugins can now add color themes, render or add actions to code blocks, and use native settings and menu components. (#61485, #61533, #61527, #61509)
  • Mobile Composer Dock: On phones and tablets, composer actions now sit in a touch dock above the keyboard with larger buttons. (#61773)
  • Scripted Runs in Worktrees: Run opencode run -w to start the session in a new git worktree, or --worktree=<name> to name it. (#60712)
  • Automatically Archived Runs: Add --archived to opencode run to keep scripted and CI sessions out of your inbox and session lists. (#61270)

Core

Improvements

  • Added opt-in MCP connection sharing through pool: true. (#61185)
  • Added opencode run --archived, which creates the session already archived so scripted and CI runs stay out of the inbox, home, and session lists. Archived sessions are also hidden from the TUI session list and skipped by --continue. (#61270)
  • Added opencode run --worktree (-w), which starts the session in a new git worktree. Use --worktree=<name> to choose the worktree name. It cannot be combined with --continue or --session. (#60712)
  • Added provider- and model-specific session warming settings, including an interval: "auto" mode based on cache TTL. (#60025, #60969)
  • Added the websearch.use policy action and switches for enabling all feature flags with OPENCODE_FLAGS=all or experimental.all_flags. (#61557, #61826)
  • Added an experimental bytecode VM for Code Mode. It is off by default; turn it on with experimental.codemode_vm: true or OPENCODE_FLAGS=codemode_vm. (#62358)
  • Added support for Microsoft Store installations of PowerShell 7 on Windows. (#61952)
  • Added Azure Messages support for Foundry Claude models. (#60934)

Bug fixes

  • opencode run now waits for the background shell commands, subagents, and image jobs that its prompt started, then lets the model respond to their results before it exits. While it waits, it prints waiting for N background job(s) (a background_wait event with --format json). There is no time limit: a job that never exits, such as a dev server or file watcher, keeps the run open until you press Ctrl+C or send SIGTERM, so set a timeout on CI jobs. (#60699, #61911)
  • Reworked noninteractive opencode run execution so arguments, cancellation, compiled WebSocket upgrades, and service shutdown complete reliably. SIGTERM now cancels the session and its background jobs and exits with code 143. (#61911, #61721, #61518, #61564)
  • Serve file reads and media from the server API as sandboxed downloads, and refuse cross-site browser navigations and requests from disallowed origins to /api/*, so a file in your project cannot run scripts on the server's origin. (#62422)
  • Webfetch and Code Mode fetch now reject URLs that contain credentials and ignore the #fragment when matching permission rules, and Code Mode fetch checks every redirect against your webfetch rules. (#60502, #61701, #62373)
  • Ask for external_directory permission before session_move leaves the current project. (#62204)
  • Wait up to 2 seconds for MCP servers that are still connecting before a model call, so tools from servers that connect quickly reach the first prompt and opencode run. Also expand ~ in local MCP working directories, handle legacy stdio servers, and send logo_uri during dynamic registration. (#60490, #61496, #61817, #59031)
  • Time out OpenCode Console config requests after 5 seconds, so startup no longer hangs when the Console accepts the connection but never answers. (#62404)
  • Retry provider requests while their host is unreachable and recover prematurely ended Cohere streams. (#61880, #61184)
  • Keep legacy custom providers whose models set only part of limit, such as only limit.context, instead of dropping the whole provider without an error. Thanks @Tarquinen. (#62240)
  • opencode acp now removes a session that a client creates with session/new and closes without prompting, so IDE integrations no longer leave empty sessions behind. (#62189)
  • Prevent repeated zsh q flags from freezing shell permission scanning. Thanks @yangguan-xue. (#60791)
  • Allow npm upgrade install scripts again. Thanks @renatomoselli. (#51554)

TUI

Improvements

  • Added Scroll on send in Settings. Choose Keep (session.submit_scroll: "keep") to retain your reading position after submitting a prompt. (#61460)

Bug fixes

  • Allow Ctrl+Z to suspend OpenCode while a form or dialog is open. (#62118)
  • Keep focus on an open dialog when a question arrives and contain plugin dialogs that throw during rendering. (#60365, #62110)
  • Show an error when Ctrl+V cannot read the clipboard. Thanks @jimmyallinai. (#61094)
  • Prevent session titles containing newlines from crashing the TUI. Thanks @vishalshekhar. (#57523)
  • Improved message spacing and prevented older history from flashing while scrolling. (#62104, #59236)
  • Jump to the first or last message with Home, End, and Ctrl+G in subagent sessions. Thanks @ricky9408. (#60817)
  • Keypad Home, End, Page Up, and Page Down now navigate the transcript like the main keys in terminals that use the kitty keyboard protocol. Thanks @argszero. (#59770)
  • opencode mini now uses the agent colors from your config. Thanks @ferdinandyb. (#62067)
  • Return to the /mcp list after signing in to an MCP server, with that server still selected. Thanks @Tarquinen. (#62245)
  • Allow favoriting models with Ctrl+F in /models when they come from a provider declared in config or an add-on instead of a saved connection. Thanks @Tarquinen. (#62244)

Desktop

Improvements

  • Added a mobile composer dock for touch devices, with 44px action targets and predictable multiline input. (#61773)
  • Added close-other-tabs and close-tabs-after actions to the tab menu. (#61631)
  • Added unread-session grouping in Inbox and failed-run indicators on tabs. (#61691, #62077)
  • MCP sign-in now accepts a pasted redirect URL when automatic redirection is unavailable. (#61706)
  • Markdown file previews now display frontmatter as a properties list. (#60333)
  • The command palette shows a date instead of "Xd ago" for sessions 7 or more days old. Hover the time to see the full date and time. (#62314)
  • Prompt history now keeps up to 200 prompts. Past the newest 100, only prompts without attachments are kept. (#62313)

Bug fixes

  • Improved prompt submission and timeline behavior during loading, reconnects, retries, scrolling, compaction, and optimistic delivery. (#62025, #62037, #61932, #62151)
  • Surface loading and offline failures in review previews, file search, details, Word pages, and review diffs instead of showing empty or clean results. (#62114, #61862)
  • Fixed provider connection, MCP sign-in, settings, and plugin loading flows that could become stuck after failures. (#62102, #61917, #61580)
  • Preserve drafts, focus, caret position, queued prompts, and selected terminal tabs across failed or concurrent transitions. (#61579, #61969, #61852)
  • Restart plugins after failed update handoffs and recover the renderer, storage, event stream, and background-service connection from transient failures. (#61649, #61908, #61804, #61748)
  • Corrected V2 file labels and mention syntax. Thanks @velmet and @cheapredwine. (#55677, #60737)
  • Attach only the @mentions in a line comment that match an existing file or folder, so a comment such as Check @here no longer fails the send. Render partial diffs in the review pane when it shows all lines, instead of a blank pane. Thanks @Tarquinen. (#62242, #62241)
  • Keep pasted images sendable after the clipboard tool deletes its temporary file. Thanks @lfscqk67. (#62057)
  • While an input method is composing text, Escape, Enter, and Tab go to the input method instead of stopping the agent or picking a suggestion. (#62318)
  • Fixed padding in the WSL distribution picker, the position of worktree and branch labels in Home search results, and the selected ring on the Interface layout setting. Thanks @inuyume. (#53676, #62317, #62309)

SDK

Improvements

  • Added client.experimental.job.list() and its Effect equivalent for listing background shell, subagent, and image jobs, with session, descendant, kind, and status filters. (#60698)
  • Server embedders can provide a custom fetch implementation for OpenCode HTTP, model, web, and remote MCP traffic. (#61582)

Extensions

Improvements

  • Added CodeBlock and CodeBlockAction GUI registries for custom fenced-code rendering and actions. (#61533)
  • GUI plugins can contribute themes through the Theme registry. (#61485)
  • Added ctx.ui.Select, settings layout components, and menu-row components for native-looking plugin interfaces. (#61527)
  • GUI plugin side panels can set, read, and persist their preferred width. (#61509)

Bug fixes

  • Isolated plugin dialogs, Markdown renderers, terminal views, lazy chunks, and artifact viewers so failures do not take down their host surface. (#62110, #62123, #61993)

paperclipai/paperclip ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v2026.1106.0 Stable2026-11-06T22:31:53Z

Paperclip v2026.1106.0

Released: 2026-11-06

Paperclip v2026.1106.0 carries 515 commits, promoted from 2026.1023.0-beta.0. The headline is that the Paperclip Runner is now the default for new agents: new Claude, Codex, OpenCode, and Pi agents start on the native Runner on qualified macOS and Linux hosts, existing agents get a one-click "Upgrade runner" action, and GitHub Copilot joins as a native harness. Around that: project repositories that automatically become skill sources, model-backed prompt injection prevention, agent observability to Braintrust or any OTLP endpoint, a unified Google Workspace connection, bulk edits on the Agents roster, workspaces that compact and expire themselves with encrypted restore points, and Grok Bot and Muse as experimental Runner providers. Underneath, server restarts report ready sooner, the Docker image boots from a precompiled bundle, task pages load faster, and more background services stop polling the database.

Breaking Changes

  • The Pipelines experiment is removed. Pipeline boards, stage settings, case automation, review queues, the pipelines CLI commands, the pipelines:write permission, the enablePipelines setting, and the linkedCases field on task detail are gone; Cases and Routines remain. Existing pipeline tables and records are preserved untouched and a stored enablePipelines value is ignored. (#16834)
  • Run failures no longer reach Sentry by default. A backend DSN alone no longer reports agent run failures. Set PAPERCLIP_SENTRY_RUN_FAILURES=unexpected to keep the previous filtered policy or all to report every failed or timed-out run; the default is off. (#16300)
  • The run history list is bounded. GET /api/companies/{companyId}/heartbeat-runs returns 200 rows by default and at most 1,000; clients that relied on an unbounded array must pass an explicit limit. (#16286)
  • paperclipai agent instructions-file:delete requires --yes like the other destructive CLI commands; scripts that call it must add the flag. (#11504)

Highlights

  • The Paperclip Runner is the default — New Claude agents on Apple Silicon, Intel macOS, and Linux x64 (#15993, #15997), new Codex agents on Apple Silicon, Intel macOS, Linux x64, and Linux ARM64 (#15683), and new OpenCode and Pi agents on macOS and Linux x64 (#17120, #17142) now start on the native Runner instead of the legacy adapter, across onboarding, the API and CLI, hires, built-ins, plugins, and imports; Linux needs GNU libc 2.39 or later, a "Legacy runner" override lives under Advanced, and existing agents keep their current runner. Existing Claude and Codex agents on qualified targets show an Upgrade runner action that converts them in one click, keeping model, account, and instructions and listing any settings that could not be converted (#17122). Claude Runner agents start with their core task tools preloaded, measured about 20% faster on simple tasks (#16885), carry the chosen effort level through resumes (#15994), and start on Linux ARM64 (#16372).
  • GitHub Copilot as a native harness — Connect GitHub Copilot through Apps / AI Connections with a saved GitHub token; the agent's model list is discovered from the authenticated account, and Copilot (CLI 1.0.88) runs through the Paperclip Runner with the same instruction, permission, and completion boundaries as the other native providers, including the Linux provider manifest needed for Daytona sandboxes (#15705, #15701, #15706).
  • Project repositories become skill sources — GitHub repositories attached to a new project are imported as skill sources automatically: Paperclip imports their skills, re-checks the configured branch every five minutes with the setup person's GitHub connection, and installs new skills unless excluded; existing projects get an "adopt repositories" action in Skills → Sources, automatic updates can be paused, project-derived skills inherit project access, and each run pins the skill versions it started with (#16066). Imports keep the whole repository snapshot at the scanned commit so skills that depend on shared scripts or sibling skills work after delivery, list every skill with its folder, and skip unsupported or oversized files with a warning instead of failing (#15731, #16308). Sources gain a branch picker that loads the repository's branches, marks the default, and validates the ref before saving (#16288); a source whose tracked branch disappeared shows a branch repair action (#16256); each saved source is listed once with grouped refresh errors (#16310); and disconnecting a source really removes it, with a keep-or-delete choice for the imported skills (#16150).
  • Model-backed prompt injection prevention — Settings → Connections lets a connection manager pick a shared OpenRouter connection and model (default openai/gpt-oss-safeguard-20b) to classify tool results before they reach an agent, replacing the regex verdict and allowing benign code that merely looks like an attack; costs appear in Costs, and a "Test model checks" action verifies two fixed samples. Off until configured. (#16976)
  • Agent observability to your own backend — Company Settings → General → Agent observability sends native Codex and Claude Code logs and traces to Braintrust (US) or a custom OTLP HTTP endpoint, with credentials held in company secrets and a test action; off by default (#16874). Braintrust also joins the Apps catalog with US and EU sign-in or API-key methods (#17141).
  • A unified Google Workspace connection — One Google Workspace entry replaces the separate Google service setups: pick Gmail, Drive (Docs, Sheets, Slides), and Calendar, each optionally read-only, and consent and tool access are bound to that selection; existing Google connections keep working (#15545). Gmail gains an opt-in archive capability (#16331).
  • Workspaces that clean up after themselves — One hour after a task tree finishes, the terminal-workspace reaper removes regenerable directories such as node_modules, dist, and target that the repository's own .gitignore ignores, keeping the branch and all source changes; the archive cooldown drops from 7 days to 1 (#16304). Opt-in Git cache expiration removes finished Paperclip-owned worktrees and private base clones 24 hours after the task tree is done and restores them at the exact commit when a task reopens (#16457, #16691); with restore points on, closed worktrees that still hold unpushed commits or uncommitted files are saved to an encrypted restore point first and restored exactly when work resumes, idle open issues are parked the same way after seven days, nested multi-repository checkouts get their own restore points, and a task whose restore point has expired reopens on a fresh workspace with a note instead of failing (#17248, #17271, #17257, #17269). Unused materialized skill versions are removed hourly and rebuilt on demand (#16433), and host-side failover backups of remote-sandbox sessions expire a week after their issues close (#16866).
  • Tasks with their own workspace — A task that needs files but has no configured source now gets a stable company/task directory instead of inheriting the agent's home folder, agents can inspect, select, and request repositories for their workspace through new runner tools, and chat connections can set project and workspace defaults (#15749); a fresh task gets its own clone when the project's default checkout contains a worktree the agent cannot read (#16095), with a one-click Retry on a clean checkout when setup is blocked that way (#16909).
  • Bulk edits on the Agents roster — Select rows and filter by harness to change the AI connection, the default model, or both for many agents at once, with a preview and retry for failed updates; the roster's status selector is removed and paused agents show a badge instead of dimming (#17215). The agent Overview shows who created an agent in companies with more than one person (#16055).
  • Grok Bot and Muse, both experimental — Grok Bot lets you invite an existing Grok Bot as an agent that receives assignments through a webhook routine and works through a dedicated agent-OAuth MCP connection, with guided setup, pairing codes, progress, questions, documents, and files flowing back into the task, and Stop and recovery controls; provider spending is not reported (#16219, #16338, #16274, #16305). Muse pairs a personal Meta Muse through a wake-only receiver and Vault-backed OAuth with a ten-minute single-use ticket, with readiness, credential expiry, and disconnect controls in the invite dialog (#16444, #16447, #16448, #16450).
  • Talk to an agent with Speko — A Speko entry in the Apps catalog connects a Speko persona to an existing agent so you can talk to it by browser voice or phone from a task, with transcripts, call controls, inbound-line settings, phone callbacks, and call entries in Activity; shown while the Chat connectors experimental setting is on (#15728).
  • Slack bots, refined — Slack connection Settings are simplified around "Open Slack configuration" and a "Send test message" row, with "Use your own app" under Advanced (#15679); plain direct messages always start or continue work even when Require at-mention is on for channel threads (#16151); and the eyes reaction appears as soon as a mention is accepted, including in direct messages (#16491, #16494).
  • A sturdier GitHub review bot — The bot keeps one main PR comment updated across pushes, ignores messages addressed to other bots, and marks stopped work accurately (#16901); summary comments are headed with the bot's display name so several bots on one PR are distinguishable (#16212); publication retries through GitHub 503/429 with bounded backoff and survives restarts without duplicate comments or checks (#16284, #16883); GitHub bot tools get the gateway's 60-second maximum so submit_review no longer times out (#16275); and settings show a "Reconnect GitHub" notice with reconnect-the-saved-App as the first option (#16856).
  • More apps in the catalog — Daytona, with personal or shared API-key credentials and 274 generated actions for sandboxes, snapshots, volumes, files, Git, and commands (#16483); Unikraft Cloud, with actions generated from its OpenAPI specs and a per-connection default metro (#17139); and Ditto (#16472). A provider card with several connections collapses them behind an "N connections" row (#16097).
  • Agent commentary in Audit — The complaints and suggestions agents file about their tooling now have a home: Audit gains a Commentary tab with agent and type filters, text search, paging, and shareable links (#16884), readable by every human role through a new commentary:view permission (#16903).

Fixes

  • Workspaces and sandboxes — A remote sandbox whose run failed before export, whose lease was cleaned up with unexported work, or whose task was cancelled before reconstruction is kept with its files behind an explicit repair decision instead of being deleted by cleanup (#16397, #16583, #16700); a task whose sandbox was destroyed before export offers a board-only "Continue from saved sources" action (#16690); files left in a stopped run's sandbox are recovered automatically and saved follow-ups delivered once (#16906); a shutdown-time workspace export keeps its ownership through the finalizer drain (#16843); a long copy-back no longer loses its lock to PostgreSQL's idle-transaction timeout (#16340, #16956); resuming a stopped reusable sandbox honors the environment's startup timeout instead of a fixed 60 seconds (#16802); a single timed-out health check no longer replaces a remote Runner (#16835); runs on repositories with thousands of historical workspaces no longer sit in "preparing" for hours (#16372, #16334, #16342, #16963); a shared checkout no longer stays blocked by a reader whose identity is gone (#16928) and a workspace blocked by another task's restricted worktree gets a clear configuration notice instead of an opaque 403 (#16545, #16895); a child task inheriting a busy shared workspace waits instead of failing with a misleading secrets error (#16371); and creating a task with the isolation selector hidden no longer fails setup (#16220).
  • Runs, recovery, and Retry — Tasks held behind a retained-workspace or failed-run hold get one reliable task-level Retry that preserves files, settles accepted results, or starts an independent workspace (#16965, #17053, #16894, #16104); Stop prevents automatic continuation until you resume (#17105, #14180); a server restart no longer strands native runs on permanent holds, repeats saved completions, or overwrites operator stops set in the meantime (#16349, #16962, #16403, #15824); legacy runs killed or lease-lost before dispatch are retried instead of settled as failed (#16355, #16358), a slow completion no longer fails a live controller (#16622), and slow unrelated queries no longer cost legacy runs their lease (#17032); a normal comment on an in-progress task no longer cancels its running retry (#16855), and stopping a queued run before it starts keeps its saved follow-up instead of running it anyway (#16357); messages for paused agents or for an agent other than the task owner are delivered once instead of being failed or deferred forever (#16679, #16673); an agent stuck marked "running" is repaired after a five-minute grace period (#16791); a Claude (ACP) run with a stalled tool call fails after 30 minutes instead of running to the four-hour timeout, and MCP calls get a 15-minute timeout (#16671, #16360); a max-turns exhaustion is no longer misclassified as a missing model (#13885); and a Codex "model at capacity" reply is retried as transient (#16407).
  • Chat and task UI — Dismissing a request above the composer clears the badge, survives reload, and no longer blocks Retry (#17218); a message to a Runner agent no longer briefly swaps its avatar for a generic "Working" row (#16989); a consumed follow-up leaves the pending queue (#16317); the final reply of a worktree task is no longer replaced by the "workspace ready" notice (#16400); a file attached before the first Agent Chat message survives task creation (#16896); task toasts in a multi-user company appear only for people involved in the task (#16153); loading a task list no longer adds unrelated finished tasks to your inbox (#16063); replies default to the latest agent that assigned you the task (#15594); side panel tabs keep a steady width (#17068, #14949); Markdown editors no longer show unsaved changes on load (#13915); and a Slack task that yields to a scheduled continuation returns its result to the original thread (#17045).
  • Costs and budgets — A budget at its warning threshold renders as a warning instead of a false "AGENT HARD STOP" banner (#16201), and budget checks no longer scale with the number of unrelated idle agents in the company (#17046).
  • Connections and MCP — One failed remote MCP call no longer makes every sibling tool on that connection unavailable (#16890); a provider 403 on one chat operation no longer disables the whole Slack, GitHub, or Discord connection (#16847, #16138); GitHub and Slack channel tools stay available on later runs of the same task (#16806); several GitHub review-thread replies each get their own follow-up run (#17054) and identical tool descriptors are listed once (#16281); a large MCP catalog refresh no longer lists a tool twice or deadlocks with an OAuth callback (#16602); assigned tool results over about 500 KB return a bounded reference and a read_tool_result tool instead of hanging (#16980); legacy Codex shell commands can see explicitly assigned service secrets (#17216); native-runner call_api follows the route's own permissions without an extra denylist (#16040); and sandboxed agents can read run history, attachments, skill files, and recovery records and send the bridge token as X-API-Key (#16575, #16892, #12785).
  • Adapters and CLI — An unlimited Claude run no longer fails its CLI probe after one second (#15329); concurrent remote Claude runs of one agent no longer break each other's skill staging (#17061); an isolated Claude task with assigned MCP tools starts (#17149); a sandbox whose preinstalled Claude CLI is older than the selected model needs gets the required version installed privately during setup instead of failing the version check (#17259); a Claude (ACP) server install no longer fails because the ACP bridge was missing from the server package (#15912); a Runner OpenCode turn survives oversized SSE events (#16401) and uses the run's managed GitHub credentials in shell commands (#16488); Hermes calls back at the adapter's configured API URL (#9425); pi_local run logs are about 150× smaller (#10249); paperclipai install and update work with npm 12 and Node 26 (#14000); paperclipai service install no longer writes a service that crash-loops on a missing managed shim (#13985); and the CLI browser login honors rate-limit Retry-After headers (#16482).
  • Server and Docker — Startup no longer fails on legacy SQL_ASCII databases (#16958) or crashes on a scheme-less publicBaseUrl (#13727); a server behind a reverse proxy with slow PTR lookups no longer stops responding for minutes after upgrading (#16333, #16335); one dropped pooled connection no longer fails permission checks, workspace readiness, or the reaper (#16857, #16859); Docker boots with many files on the data volume or a non-default USER_UID/USER_GID no longer walk the whole volume (#15992, #16475); both shipped Compose files forward PAPERCLIP_TOOL_ACTION_SIGNING_SECRET (#15202); a Stop that outlasts sync-back returns a retryable 409 instead of a 500 (#16032); concurrent task title edits no longer 500 (#13898); one malformed comment ID no longer blocks a task's queued wakes (#13912); watchdog wakes deliver the operator's instructions (#13771); the child-task cap counts only open children (#11542); a plain-heartbeat agent can comment on tasks again (#12621); a human handoff from a legacy run no longer gets cancelled by a slow restore (#16082); and feedback exports keep public identifiers readable while still masking real tokens (#16168).

Improvements

  • Experimental Cases keep one primary task across agent handoffs with a durable handoff ledger, native create_case, read_case, update_case, and handoff_case tools, read-only case pages, and reusable named workflow prompts a case adopts at kickoff or on handoff (#16959, #17114).
  • A failed agent-lifecycle verification names the failed step and a corrective action (#16418), and onboarding creates the agent record as soon as you submit its name, so setup and retries keep one identity (#17005).
  • Plugins can subscribe to committed project and agent lifecycle events with replay instead of polling (#16203); receive agent configuration, instruction, skill, connection, tool, and grant changes through durable runtime hooks (#16499, #16501, #16603, #16604, #16605); store agent- and project-owned instance secrets in the encrypted secret store (#16191, #17034); own project storage and task workspaces, act as the default workspace provider, and run native Runner tasks in plugin-owned environments verified by the agent environment test (#17001, #17247, #16978, #17029, #17252, #15406); and declare authenticated WebSocket routes and agent detail views (#17111).
  • More background work waits for committed writes and known deadlines instead of polling the database: Telegram maintenance (#16094), terminal workspace cleanup (#16282), login cleanup (#17106), execution-control recovery (#17094), MCP event subscriptions (#17036), and skill-source sync (#17008); routines and task monitors fire at their saved deadline instead of waiting for the next scan (#16514).
  • Faster starts everywhere: the server reports ready sooner after a restart because housekeeping runs after readiness and settled finalizations are skipped (#16329, #16485, #16328); the Docker image boots from a precompiled runtime bundle, cutting code-load time from up to 24 seconds to under 4 (#16672); task pages load lazily, defer hidden rendering, and refresh the recent-tasks sidebar through one bounded request (#16849, #16878, #16851, #16862); and agent launches overlap GitHub, skill, tool, and sandbox preparation, reuse a per-company Git object cache and verified sandbox image files, and make far fewer database and Daytona calls (#16620, #16608, #16585, #16487, #16687, #16493, #16683, #16536, #16521, #16527, #16561, #16468, #16480, #16552, #16484, #16473, #16425, #16424, #16461, #16498, #16576, #16569, #16414, #17026).
  • Tasks, project lists, inbox discovery, and Blocked hide agent- and system-created tasks by default behind a "show agent-created tasks" filter saved per company (#16157), and Inbox "Mine" excludes channel, routine, and agent-created tasks unless you commented, were assigned, or have an open request (#16356); failure notices show only the latest error with its retry action (#16982); and an expired connection request card says what is still not connected (#14067).
  • An experimental Artifacts V1 presentation shows paper stacks and tiles on the Artifacts page, compact rows in the panel, and grouped rows in the thread (#17228); you can upload files directly in the Artifacts panel by icon or drag-and-drop without sending a message (#15964); the offline recovery page matches the app (#16904); the "Run with provider trace" header button is replaced by the agent's Debugging configuration (#17211); and an app's Access tab shows linked users' photos (#15577).
  • Adding a repository skill to an agent no longer stalls on a full library read (#16899), and invalid arguments to assigned tools, call_api, and set_task_monitor return schema locations and repair guidance (#16905, #16889, #16868, #16961).
  • paperclip_runner agents can set homeMode: "preconfigured" to run native Codex or ACPX Claude with the execution account's existing login, skills, and MCP configuration (#16486); the Runner reports refreshed OAuth credentials without token values crossing the protocol (#16463) and can register verified session-local stdio MCP servers on Unix (#16526); the task PATCH API accepts expectedStatusVersion for backlog-to-todo promotion (#16893); and an anonymous GET /api/liveness reports process liveness without touching the database (#16101).
  • Failure reports record why an OpenCode turn fails when its server stops (#16863); Sentry reports for run failures link to the task and run pages, skip classified provider quota limits, and report a native retry only when the retry ends (#16803, #16029, #16865); due task monitors held by recovery record why in the task activity (#16891); and, when an OTLP endpoint is configured, traces gain spans for controller preparation, execution configuration, workspace and connector preparation, and finalization (#16178, #16236, #16419, #16428, #16454, #16515).

Upgrade Guide

  • Migrations 0334–0374 run on upgrade. Additive schema: 0334, 0341, and 0342 repository skill snapshots, project_skill_sources, and import budgets; 0335 chat_slack_manager_grants and its link from Slack registrations; 0336–0338 execution workspace repository tables; 0339 and 0370 agent-, plugin-, and project-owned company_secrets; 0344 and 0346 Grok Bot tables; 0349–0353 Muse tables; 0357 company_observability; 0362 prompt injection prevention tables; 0363–0368 and 0371 case handoff and workflow tables; 0372–0374 execution_workspace_restore_points; 0373 interaction dismissal columns. Constraint rewrites: 0340 allows delete as a lifecycle-event action; 0361 allows github as an AI default provider. Triggers: 0347, 0348, and 0354–0356 install runtime-invalidation triggers on agents, skills, connections, grants, memberships, and secrets; 0358 bumps issues.status_version on assignee change; 0372 expires restore points when issues close. Row updates: 0343 backfills agents.created_by_user_id from the activity log; 0347 seeds one agent_runtime event per active agent; 0359 grants commentary:view to every active human membership; 0363 backfills case_access_sources. Indexes: 0360 adds a partial index for retained-workspace recovery; 0345 and 0369 are built CONCURRENTLY outside a transaction and are safe to rerun if interrupted. Expect 0343, 0345, and 0369 to take longer on instances with large run histories, and the check-constraint rewrites in 0339, 0362, and 0370 to scan company_secrets, budget_reservations, and cost_events.
  • New agents default to the Paperclip Runner (#15993, #15997, #15683, #17120, #17142): on qualified hosts (Apple Silicon, Intel macOS, Linux x64 with glibc 2.39+, and Linux ARM64 for Codex), new Claude, Codex, OpenCode, and Pi agents are created on the native Runner regardless of the enableNativeRunner setting. Existing agents are unchanged; choose "Legacy runner" under Advanced to opt a new agent out, or use Upgrade runner on an existing agent's configuration to convert it (#17122).
  • New environment variables, none required. Workspace cleanup: PAPERCLIP_WORKSPACE_COMPACTION_ENABLED (on unless exactly false) and PAPERCLIP_WORKSPACE_COMPACTION_DELAY_HOURS (default 1) control removal of regenerable files from finished task worktrees, and PAPERCLIP_WORKSPACE_REAPER_COOLDOWN_DAYS now defaults to 1 instead of 7 (#16304); PAPERCLIP_GIT_CACHE_EXPIRATION_ENABLED and PAPERCLIP_GIT_BASE_EXPIRATION_ENABLED (default false) with PAPERCLIP_GIT_CACHE_EXPIRATION_DRY_RUN (default true) opt into expiring finished worktrees and base clones (#16457); PAPERCLIP_GIT_RESTORE_POINTS_ENABLED (default false) with PAPERCLIP_GIT_RESTORE_POINT_TERMINAL_DELAY_DAYS (default 3), PAPERCLIP_GIT_RESTORE_POINT_IDLE_DAYS (default 7, 0 disables), and PAPERCLIP_GIT_RESTORE_POINT_RETENTION_DAYS (default 90) govern encrypted restore points (#17248, #17271, #17241); PAPERCLIP_SKILL_VERSION_RETENTION_ENABLED (default true) with _DRY_RUN (false), _KEEP (0), and _GRACE_MINUTES (60) prunes unused materialized skill versions (#16433); PAPERCLIP_NATIVE_HARNESS_BACKUP_EXPIRATION_ENABLED (default true) with _DAYS (7) and _DRY_RUN (false) expires remote-session failover backups, so reopening an issue closed longer than that window starts a fresh provider session (#16866). Reporting: PAPERCLIP_SENTRY_RUN_FAILURES (off, unexpected, or all; default off) (#16300). Claude agents also honor an existing ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL and ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS (#16850).
  • New experimental settings, all off by default: Grok Bot (enableGrokBot), which requires Assistant connections (MCP), the Paperclip Runner, authenticated sign-in, and an HTTPS PAPERCLIP_PUBLIC_URL (#16219); Muse — Personal agent (enableMuse), which requires the Runner and public HTTPS (#16450); Artifacts V1 (enableArtifactsV1) (#17228). Speko voice appears under the existing Chat connectors setting (#15728) and workflows under Cases (#17114). enablePipelines is removed (#16834). Settings → Connections is now always visible and no longer depends on Experimental fast responses (#16976).
  • commentary:view for every human role (#16903): migration 0359 and startup seeding grant the new permission to every active human membership, including viewers, so anyone in the company can open Audit → Commentary. Deleting that grant from a member is temporary: startup seeding re-adds every role-default grant for each active human member on the next server start, and this release has no durable way to withhold it. Source-task privacy still filters what each person sees in commentary.
  • Sentry operators (#16300): set PAPERCLIP_SENTRY_RUN_FAILURES=unexpected to keep receiving run-failure events; they are off by default even with SENTRY_DSN_BACKEND configured.
  • API clients: the run history list is capped at 1,000 rows (default 200) (#16286); the linkedCases field and all Pipeline routes are gone (#16834); a PATCH that replaces executionPolicy and sets done during a live review cycle returns 422 unexecuted_review_stage (#13178); and anything parsing GitHub review summary headings must accept bot names (#16212).
  • Custom Docker images (#16672): the shipped image now starts with node server/dist-runtime/index.mjs; images that invoked server/dist/index.js through tsx should switch to the runtime bundle.
  • Default views change: agent- and system-created tasks are hidden from task lists until you enable "show agent-created tasks" (#16157), and Inbox "Mine" no longer includes automation you did not take part in (#16356).
  • Google Workspace (#15545): new Google Workspace requests depend on the Paperclip-hosted OAuth broker update, and older brokers reject them; existing per-service Google connections keep working.
  • Harness pins are unchanged from v2026.1023.0: Codex 0.160.0, Claude Agent SDK 0.3.286 (Claude Code 2.1.286), and OpenCode 1.18.34. GitHub Copilot ships at CLI 1.0.88 (#15701).
  • No other configuration or API changes require action.

Automattic/harper ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v3.1.0 Stable2026-11-06T22:24:06Z

What's Changed

Howdy! Today, I'm starting a new tradition for Harper. Instead of the bland, colorless release notes you've been receiving for past releases, you'll now be getting these new, awesome release notes I type up myself.

The hope is that by writing these notes directly, in addition to the updates on my blog, I can get closer to you guys, the people using Harper. I'll share some of the things on my mind, the places Harper is going, and the wonderful work Harper's community has gotten done since the last release.

We have a ton of updates and bug fixes coming in this release. I'll cover some of my favorite changes, but I won't be able to cover everything. You can find a list of ALL the changes in the PR notes at the end.

First off, we've simplified some parts of Harper's UI. In the desktop app, instead of seeing a green or red bar to signify if Harper is activated, you should see a dot below the Harper logo in the menu if the service is running. If the service is not running, there's no dot. Simple enough. You'll also observe that the menu bar should look much nicer in both light and dark modes. In the Chrome Extension, we've reverted some of the changes from v3.0.0 that made the popup menu (what you get when you click on the Harper logo) more "exciting". They were distracting and a little confusing, so we banished them.

Secondly, I've done a ton of work fixing bugs in our support for Google Docs. You should now see correct behavior when Harper checks sentences that span multiple pages and tables should now play more nicely with suggestions. Plus, thanks to @mvanhorn, we have some improved support for editors like WordPress' Gutenberg.

In the desktop app, we're pushing out some similar improvements. We got some reports that Harper didn't play well with setups with multiple monitors. It should be much cleaner now.

We also got some feedback that the Desktop app was a little confusing. We heard similar feedback a while ago about the Chrome Extension, which we addressed with a tip system. We set a similar "tip" system up for the Desktop app. We shall see if it help make things a little less confusing. From now on, you'll occasionally see tips appear when you review a highlighted problem in your text.

The most important changes, as always, were in our core grammar engine. We've added a ton of new rules and fixed a number of broken ones. Thanks to everyone who opened PRs in that vein, especially @hippietrail who's been reviewing them so prodigiously.

I'll talk to you again in the next release! Thanks for using Harper. Have a great weekend.

Best, Elijah Potter (@elijah-potter)

coreyhaines31/marketingskills ↗ 2 releases · 2 stable
v2.14.2v2.14.2 — Organic short-form growth for apps and products: earn posting volume with views, and pick… Stable2026-11-06T17:52:58Z

Organic short-form growth for apps and products: earn posting volume with views, and pick formats by what they produce.

  • social (2.4.1 → 2.4.2): the short-form guide replaces the flat "2-4 posts a day to grow" table with a staged cadence (warm up, 1 thoughtful post a day until posts clear a view floor, up to 3 a day spaced 3-4 hours apart, then another warmed account rather than more posts per account, and a pause rule for stalled accounts). Adds a new-account warm-up checklist with compliance guardrails (brand-affiliated accounts, no bought or automated accounts, disclosed paid creators), a winning-format search that logs tests and calls winners on signups or installs rather than views, how to measure what views produce, and post-once-publish-everywhere.
  • reverse-engineer-marketing (1.1.0 → 1.1.1): the mobile apps pack adds account networks as a signal (unbranded niche accounts that share the app's link, with first post dates, cadence, and the format repeated across them) and a play for remaking their winning format.
  • marketing-loops (1.7.0 → 1.8.0): new format-finding loop that reads posts at 48 hours, applies the staged cadence per account, names the winning format by signups, and sends cadence increases and new accounts to a human. The catalog now has 60 loops.
  • Tools: new Post Bridge integration guide (REST API, official MCP and CLI, multi-account posting, per-post daily view analytics), listed alongside Buffer; the Social Media recommendation in the registry is now neutral.

Skill ZIP assets

Complete source catalog: 53 skills. Claude web target: 53 skills with reviewed short descriptions. Perplexity Computer target: 53 skills.

Choose <name>-claude-web.zip for the documented single-folder web upload format. Choose <name>-perplexity.zip for Perplexity Computer (SKILL.md at the archive root). Source ZIPs retain original metadata; they are not substitutes for omitted web ZIPs. Live client upload, account limits, and invocation behavior have not been verified.

Web omissions (also recorded in claude-web-manifest.json):

  • None.

Landscape mode (references/landscape.md): compare profiles three to five competitors from outside (homepage scripts, marketing email platforms and ad-account verifications from DNS, sitemap page families, marketing roles on public job boards) and sorts the signals into table stakes, single-company bets, and the user's gaps (--you), with a CSV matrix. Where they're weak (references/weak-spots.md, a new section in stage 8): complaint themes from 1-2 star reviews, switching posts, price rises, abandoned search families, and markets left, each routed to a play (alternative pages, switching offers, positioning, battle cards), with rules for truthful comparative claims and trademarks. New script commands: jobs (Greenhouse, Lever, and Ashby boards: roles by function, marketing channels being hired for, tools named in posts, languages, first-marketing-hire wording), reviews (App Store reviews by month and the terms that skew to low ratings, or any exported review CSV with --from, reviewer names dropped), and adoption (monthly npm and PyPI downloads and GitHub star history as a free growth curve for developer tools). Watch mode adds a launch radar for clustered signals. Contract tests for every new parser and calculation; four new evals.

EpicGames/lore ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v0.13.0 Stable2026-11-06T16:12:09Z

The latest release of Lore, Epic Games' next-generation open source version control system.

Quickstart | Design | Documentation | Discord

Quick Install

Windows

irm https://raw.githubusercontent.com/EpicGames/lore/main/scripts/install.ps1 | iex

macOS / Linux

curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/EpicGames/lore/main/scripts/install.sh | bash

Release Notes

Changes since v0.12.0.

Breaking changes

  • lore.user.v1: UserService partition RPCs are replaced by RepositoryRegistryService; with user_url set, the directory there must serve it before lore-server is upgraded
  • lore-server authorization: changing a partition needs write, changing branch protection needs owner or admin (or write for the creator), and presigned URLs need presign instead of is_service_account
  • Server hooks: Hook::pre_handler is now async; a timeout fails the request with UNAVAILABLE instead of INTERNAL
  • Clients on a lores:// remote refuse advertised endpoints that don't authenticate the server, such as lore://
  • Ignore and view rules match paths as .gitignore does: ** and character classes stay within one path component
  • .loreignore applies only to untracked paths, so changes to committed files it matches are reported and staged (ADR-00027)
  • lore sync --reset and lore branch switch --reset leave untracked files in place, and lore reset no longer consults the ignore file
  • A failed link push exits with its error's code instead of 255, or the new code 52 (LinkDivergent)
  • File names Windows reads as something else, such as C:, file. or file::$DATA, are refused on every platform

Features

  • Progress is printed every 30 seconds when stdout is not a terminal (LORE_PROGRESS_INTERVAL)
  • lore auth login --reuse-session reuses a still-valid login
  • The AuthUserToken event reports when the stored OIDC session expires
  • [sync] prefetch_tree = true fetches a sync's tree in bulk requests
  • lore-server: server.trust_unsigned_identity names callers in locks and notifications on servers without auth
  • lore-server: the lock service's Query can be forwarded to a remote server (lock_query)
  • lore-server: configurable QUIC initial congestion window (initial_cwnd_bytes) and, on Linux, several UDP sockets per endpoint (num_sockets)

Fixes & Improvements

  • Much faster scans under large views and ignore files (157 s to 11 s for a 6,342-rule view over 450,000 files)
  • Whole-directory adds and deletes in a sync run on every core, and large syncs use less memory
  • Windows: fewer file system calls for antivirus and other filter drivers to inspect
  • Written files take fewer stats and only change executable bits where needed
  • Throttled storage copies back off and retry instead of uploading the full payload
  • gRPC over TLS falls back to bundled root certificates when the OS store has none
  • Sparse views: excluded directories are skipped by clone, diff and scans, and created only for content the view keeps
  • Scans and staging add or delete excluded directories only along with content below them
  • Directory-only rules such as Intermediate/ apply to directories only
  • lore status lists and lore unstage takes back staged changes to ignored paths
  • Fixed lore stage --scan taking in some ignored files under nested re-inclusions
  • Fixed scans keeping untracked entries an ignore rule later excludes
  • Fixed lore stage taking in every file of a new directory where only some were marked
  • Merges keep both a move and an edit of the same file, and report conflicts for paths with [ or ]
  • Case-only renames commit without --force
  • Fixed deleted directories left behind when a file in them is spelled in another case
  • Merges under a sparse view show up in diffs, file history and lore link list
  • Fixed several staging, unstaging and reset problems with links and layers
  • lore reset keeps nested repositories
  • Fixed a failed removal on Unix making files world-writable; removals no longer follow symbolic links
  • Lore service closes its SWFS mounts on exit, and on Windows a second service no longer takes over the socket
  • lore repository instance prune keeps unreadable instances on file systems such as Btrfs
  • Revisions named by number resolve faster, and common-ancestor lookups no longer return a too-new revision
  • lore-server: faster branch push and BranchList, and the legacy BranchPush handles deleted branches as v1 does
  • lore-server: replicated store requests no longer stall during a client refresh, and composite reads fall back to the durable store sooner
  • lore-aws: shared DynamoDB reads, and batch reads back off on unprocessed keys
  • lore-server: more branch push metrics under lore.branch_push

Release Assets

[!IMPORTANT] These can be downloaded under the "Assets" section below.

Installer

  • lore-setup - Lore CLI Windows installer

Binaries

  • lore - Lore CLI
  • loreserver - Lore Server
  • liblore - Lore Shared Library

Headers

  • lore.h - Single header for liblore (packaged with liblore)

Supported Platforms

  • Windows x86_64
  • macOS AArch64
  • Linux x86_64
  • Linux AArch64
  • Linux AArch64, optimized for Graviton/Neoverse 512-tvb

Lore Desktop Client

[!IMPORTANT] Lore Desktop Client is available as binaries only, download the installer for your platform here:

  • Windows: Lore-Desktop-Setup-x64.exe
  • macOS: Lore-Desktop-arm64.dmg
  • Linux (Ubuntu 24.04): lore-desktop_amd64.deb
  • Linux (AppImage): Lore-Desktop-x86_64.AppImage

⬇️ Open Source Lore Assets Below ⬇️

First-time preview setup

Existing stable versions do not support --channel. Download the archive for your OS and architecture from this release, verify it against SHA256SUMS, and extract the entire archive into its own directory (keep all Linux companion files together). Run the extracted binary explicitly, using its full path, rather than the old stable jcode on PATH. For Windows, use the extracted .exe.

From that preview binary, run jcode update --channel preview (substitute its full path for jcode) to persist the opt-in channel and receive future previews. To roll back, run the preview binary with update --channel stable, or reinstall the current stable release. Stable users are never moved to previews automatically.

This preview series ends when the v1.0.0 stable tag is created.
